ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e7331	We now reach 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Baumgartner-Gabitzer is speaking on the floor. Your welcome time is 8 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e7360	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Today, in the first item on the agenda, Parliament is dealing with the popular desire “Austria remains free!”. This popular desire had 258,281 declarations of support and was thus based on the 21st century. Place of 32 popular desires previously held. At the outset, I would like to address the accusation raised in the Committee that this popular desire would be shubbled. That is not exactly the case, quite the contrary. We deal with every popular desire very intensively. We have also discussed very detailed, partly controversial, partly consistent, and have, of course, dealt with all things in detail. That is why I must expressly reject this accusation! Both the Committee and the Assembly are always concerned with all popular desires carried out in Austria. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and the Freeman – BZÖ.]] However, I would like to accuse the proponents of this popular desire that the objectives set therein represent a very populist access to these issues – topics that are extremely important and whose treatment is missing in the form. The first goal is to preserve Austrian neutrality. – It is best to realize that this is a very big populism behind it. The fact is that we have a neutrality law, the Federal Constitutional Act of 26. In October 1955, and no substantial force in this Parliament is thinking of amending this Federal Constitutional Act. As we all know, a two-thirds majority need to be changed to a federal constitution law. Therefore, the first goal of this popular desire is simply and simply missing. The second objective is to promote Turkey's accession to the EU. – This takes on a lot of fears of the population and is also something that we certainly still have to deal with very closely in future. At the moment, however, it is far too early to decide on such things – not to talk, but to decide. Of course, we need to discuss how Turkey's accession to the EU will be manageable. We from the People's Party have proposed a very clear way: We are currently in a negotiation process with Turkey. In a negotiation process, one usually has to wait for the result. And if there are results of negotiations, we will work to ensure that there is a referendum on Turkey's accession to the EU. This is the most politically clear and clear opinion and the only right one from the process. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The third objective of this popular desire is the defence of the EU Constitution in its present form. – We of the ÖVP think this approach is simple and simply wrong. If you are dealing with the EU Constitution – the House has done so several times – you know and see that this EU Constitution is an attempt to further develop the EU. This is a work put into a treaty, which has led to a great many preparatory actions of very many nationalities. The attempt to give the European Union a constitution is correct and important. Whether or not any point contained in this Constitution also finds the support of all of us, is of course worthy of discussion. However, it is also in the nature of the fact that there are points in such a comprehensive but necessary work, which ultimately leads to the strengthening of all important EU institutions, which do not find the support of all. Nevertheless, the Constitution has succeeded well. Austria was able to participate and has also contributed to this. By the way, it is also here – and this is always our heart; we have said this in every discussion on the EU Constitution – that it is an extension of civil rights. This too gives every opportunity to agree. That is why this third goal is simply not a right one. I would like to take a brief look at the reasons for this popular desire, which I strongly reject. The explanatory statement states that “sitting in Parliament really represents the people”, so it is spoken here of by our Members – this “a subservient attitude towards the Brussels Eurocrats” is underset, “a cowardly schilling abroad, a greater identification with the megalomania than with the needs of Austria”. First of all, I find it incredible from the language that and how this is accused of us. I do not know anyone in this House who is more identified with the EU than with Austria. I would like to expressly say this. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I know from many discussions that we have held in my own group, but also here in plenary that there is no political group and no Members who put the needs of the EU higher than the needs of Austrian citizens. That is why it is also a popular desire from the language with which I cannot identify myself in any way. The path of Austria to the EU, the way of Austria towards opening up, the way to a larger community was the only right way for a small economy, for a small country. It was and are many opportunities associated with it – only for young people when you think about exchange programmes and other opportunities. It was an opening, an extension that was not always easy. But the often difficult path has been worthwhile. We have learned a lot, the economy has benefited, the youth learns from it – and we will stay in this way. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e7451	The next speaker is Mr Gusenbauer. 10 minutes welcome. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e7473	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! It is a good opportunity today to discuss the results of the Committee's deliberations on this popular desire in the National Council, because it is good today to point out a few things that should actually be out of dispute. First: There is no need to go beyond the existing constitutional guarantee of neutrality. The Austrian neutrality is in the Constitution, it is the practice of our foreign policy, and there is no reason to change anything about this neutrality. All the political groups in this House should be in favour of this, ladies and gentlemen. [[It is a pleasure to see the SPÖ as well as the Greens and the ÖVP.]] It is rather the question of what you are talking about, because, in the event of a change or the abolition of neutrality, you can of course ask questions about the means by which it should be abolished. But if no-one has any intention of abolishing neutrality, then we are up-to-date with what is in the Austrian federal constitution. I would like to say: It has shown in recent years how important it is that Austria continues to pursue its neutrality policy. It is true that with neutrality policy it is not possible to prevent every war... [[Abg. No one can be prevented!]] You can do a lot in advance, but unfortunately you can't prevent every war. But if there are wars, you do not have to participate in every war as a neutral state. And in any case, it is preferable that, when unjust wars take place, that is, those who do not have the legitimacy of the World Security Council – that is an armed commitment – are not involved in such military conflicts. [[Abg. Scheibner: Is there a “righteous” war?]] [[Abg. Scheibner: This is not a war!]] Others may do so, for Austria, neutrality excludes participation in military conflicts, and so it should remain! [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. May. Lunacek.]] At the same time, however, when we take neutrality seriously, we face military conflicts everywhere, where there is no legitimacy by the World Security Council. It is also part of the neutrality that we are setting ourselves up there, where we believe that international law is being broken. I believe that precisely these days are well suited to the American President, if he is already at the EU summit in Vienna, to say clearly that Austria, the Austrian population and the Austrian Parliament consider the war in Iraq, which does not have the coverage of the World Security Council, to be false, that this war must be ended quickly, and that the peaceful reconstruction of Iraq should be carried out as quickly as possible. I think this is an important message, my dear friends. Ladies and gentlemen! [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. May. Lunacek.]] On top of that, it turns out that all the reasons put forward for this Iraq war have proved to be wrong. There have been two reasons for this war by the President of the United States. He meant that first of all there is the hort of terror and second of Iraq has weapons of mass destruction. In the meantime, it is clear that the hort of terror is in many areas of the Middle East, but not in Iraq, and that no weapons of mass destruction could be found there either. [[Inter-call]] This has also led to the fact that in the meantime the American government has changed its argumentation and no longer says it's about the weapons of mass destruction and it's about the war against terror, no, it's about the peaceful or less peaceful regime change in Iraq. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen, the question of the change of regime is an interesting question: Where anywhere in the world is there supposed to be a regime change? And on what basis do you think that the respective regime is the worst, most humane dictatorship in the world, and why – derived from it – is there a reason for the international community or individuals to intervene? Is not the truth, rather, that unfortunately there are still a number of dictatorships in the world with humane regimes, partly even worse than in Iraq, where is it looked away? [[Abg. Wheeler: Yes, sir!]] Actually, the reason is, one must say that there is a change of regime, in reality a proposed argument, which should represent a legitimacy for other strategic objectives. Why do I say that? – Because it is important that, after Austria has decided not to take part in it, because it is a neutral state, it has proved to be the right one in retrospect. And in fact, all those who have long wanted to banish neutrality in history's taboos would have to produce and say: Let us be glad that we have preserved neutrality, for so we must not engage in such senseless, false adventures! – This is an essential teaching! [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. May. Lunacek.]] [[Abg. Scheibner: Please be a little more serious in the argument!]] Mr Scheibner is still speaking of the former Minister of Defence, also, as it were, a very glowing follower of NATO. [[Abg. Scheibner: A little more serious in the discussion is not forbidden!]] [[Abg. Scheibner: Just like the cap!]] That's what you have to say: Again and again it has been said that there is no need for neutrality, since all Europeans will go to NATO. – The Iraq war in particular has also shown the disunity among the NATO countries. If we are talking in Europe today about the need for Europe to act more with one voice in the world, this is not only the problem between the NATO Member States and the non-Nato Member States, but also within NATO itself, a different opinion on such an essential issue as the Iraq war. One can discuss matters of common foreign policy in detail, but the basic issues of foreign policy are those concerning the decision between war and peace, namely, one chooses to participate in a military debate together, or not. And the truth is that neither within the European Union nor within NATO has come to a common view on this issue. This is at the heart of the external crisis both of the European Union and of NATO. It is therefore entirely legitimate to discuss how to improve the mechanisms within the European Union in order to achieve a common foreign policy. But as long as there is no willingness to bring individual strategic individual interests into the common interest of the European Union, a common European foreign policy will probably be condemned to fail on such central issues as war and peace. This popular desire or discussion in the Committee also raised the question of a European Constitution – this is an interesting question that we have during the Austrian EU Presidency during the last year, we say so once, at least accompanied, after the previous Constitutional Treaty has to be regarded as a failure, since the referendums in France and the Netherlands have failed. Now, everyone expects a new start – perhaps after the next elections in France – to take place. But it is already the question of what lessons one actually learns from this failure. And these lessons can be started at different levels. The first level is: Is what is contained in this Constitution sufficient to achieve a European consensus or, for some, too much, because more politics is formulated there than they would like in the Constitution? This is a very essential question, which has hardly been discussed since the failure. If you think that there is not enough inside, you have to say: The political objectives of the European Union need to be more precise and above all the social Europe formulated. However, if one considers that Europe is overly demanding and individual Member States are not prepared to provide precise policy descriptions, then one must conclude the opposite and say: All right, we need European fundamental rights, we need rules for the functioning of the institutions in Europe, voting mechanisms, but the political objectives which are often not found in the constitutions of the individual nation states are excluded from the Constitution. It is then the matter of the respective majority relations within the European Union, in the Council, in the Parliament, in the Commission, which policies are being made. – This is a principle decision to be made. The second basic decision to be made – and here too, I believe, should one be wise from the mistakes of the past – is how such a constitution should be adopted. I think that this "vote" is a false access, because that means nothing but that it has the majority in a single Member State in hand to bring the whole thing to a failure. This means that, in the extreme case, the European Union depends on whether the majority agree or not in Luxembourg. That would be about as if a single part of the whole could be blocked by the adoption of a constitution of another form. I think it would have been more reasonable from the outset – with a new constitutional text one should go this way – to say: There should be a pan-European vote on such a European Constitutional Treaty, which works in accordance with the principle of the double majority. In other words, there must be a majority in the majority of Member States and a majority in order to be democratically accepted by all European citizens. I believe that this would be an important conclusion from the failure of the current constitutional process. I think we would be well advised to take this path if this constitutional process regained new momentum next year – and not the ratification of individual states in Parliament and referendums in other countries. Turkey's accession is an exciting question, because at present one is given the impression that even within Turkey the question is whether Turkey wants to become a member or not is open again. Firstly, it is not clear whether Turkey will recognise every member country of the European Union before entering a crucial phase. But that is an essential condition. It is absolutely impossible to think that someone will become a member of the European Union, who is not willing to recognise each of the Member States so far. So I think this must be out of the question. It is also clear that the existing Treaty elements of the European Union must be accepted by each new Member State – therefore also from Turkey. I believe that this negotiation process is more open than ever before, as no movement is taking place on the crucial issues. Should a negotiated conclusion occur, there is a widespread consensus in Austria that the people should vote on it. But I actually see this day in great distance and not in the near future.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e7759	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Let me start by mentioning a point that is very important for the Greens. Ladies and gentlemen, we only want to confirm this: by including this announcement from you into the federal constitution! In doing so, we want to make it clear to the Austrian people that we are serious about this. Whether a Wolfgang Schüssel, who made this announcement, will still bear political responsibility in ten or 15 years, is highly unlikely. Ladies and gentlemen, the Presidency's task, Mr Winkler, I would like to tell you, would have been to implement the results of the so-called Turkey Summit last year, which you also quoted, into reality. Have it back then. She negotiates – I welcomed this – that it will be about the European Union's capacity to accept when it comes to discussions on Turkey's accession. But this aspect of acceptability is, as we had to hear, rejected by the other Member States, and this will not be a criterion in relation to Turkey's arbitrary accession. They also negotiated the so-called stop button at the time. But this stop button seems to have been just a placebo for the critical public, because there would have been dozens of reasons to press this stop button, Mr State Secretary: The human rights situation in the Republic of Turkey, its behaviour towards an EU Member, namely Cyprus, has already been addressed by previous speakers; I do not need to repeat that. The Austrian Presidency's task would have been to prevent Mr Winkler, State Secretary, from starting negotiations with Turkey, not unfounded, not because we want to live out some resentments, but because this country is not only ready to be admitted to the EU at the moment, but is not ready for serious accession negotiations with it. Ladies and gentlemen, third point: the EU Constitution. We liberals do not act against further development of the European constitutional situation, but we must say one thing again and again clearly: Any change in the Austrian constitutional situation can only happen after a referendum in Austria! That is why I find this proposal for a Europe-wide referendum to be missing, because a Europe-wide referendum can only be argumentable and democratically legitimised if the same outcome is achieved in all Member States. But how do you explain to the Austrians who may vote against a new EU Constitution why this Constitution should enter into force in Austria, because the other Member States have been in favour of it? Ladies and gentlemen, a referendum on the new European constitutional situation can, in my opinion, only be democratically legitimized by national referendums in all Member States. These referendums are necessary in order to force the European level to translate all the contents of such a constitution into citizens in order to make European political content closer to citizens. That is why a referendum on these issues is necessary. Ladies and gentlemen, these three points contained in this popular desire are very important. Today's debate has shown us this. I can announce to you that during the next legislative period, we will, of course, continue to develop all these points not only further but also further. – Thank you very much.

I think this is a very important step, because we have been confronted for years with the opinion: You are constantly adopting new laws; when do you remove obsolete laws? With this step, ladies and gentlemen, with this 2006 deregulation law, if I have counted correctly, eleven federal constitutional regulations, 56 simple laws and 140 regulations are repealed. This is a step in the same way as the efforts at European level, because the Vice-President of the European Commission, Verheugen, meant that he had the task of reducing the administrative burden of EU businesses by 25 percent over the next few years. He said at the great conference in St. Pölten, which was also about subsidiarity, that one must also have the courage to fall short. I now know that it is very difficult for our legal system to have a gap, but I believe that the courage to re-establish obsolete legislation is very difficult. I think you should have. And we from the government parties have this courage, ladies and gentlemen. We prove this with this deregulation law. Mr Finz, I am very glad that this should only be the first signal, I am very glad that we have actually taken the lead here in another European country, namely Holland, where, according to a standard cost model, there is the objective of reducing also 25 percent of the administrative burden of businesses. In Austria, the cost of 2 billion € would be reduced by unnecessary bureaucracy and regulation, it would mean a 1.5 percent growth rate of gross social product – at a time when we all think about how to promote growth, i.e. a 1.5 percent growth rate of gross social product – and it would increase labour productivity by 1.7 percent. Mr. State Secretary Dr. Finz, I know it's primarily your area in the department. Therefore: Thank you for what you have done so far, thank you for those choices that have been made, and thank you also for your intention to reduce these costs by 25 percent by 2010. It is in the interests of growth, employment and jobs in Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e10646	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! ==References== The century presents new challenges to us, and globalisation brings enormous opportunities to a country like Austria, which, of course, we use actively – this shows the Federal Government; for example, we are European champions in export; every second euro is due to Exports of goods and services have been achieved, from which every second job in Austria, especially in small and medium-sized enterprises, now benefits, but globalisation also brings new challenges, such as increased international competition, possible departures from companies or the issue of global respect for human rights and social standards. A modern state must react to this, to achieve positive effects on society as a whole. A modern state must provide a framework at regional, national and international level in which work, corporate spirit, creativity and innovation can develop for the benefit of all. All these tasks and many other tasks can only be carried out by a state whose budget is on healthy financial legs focused on the core of its social system and which benefits modern technologies for a slim and efficient administration. And he has to retreat where he slows down or threatens to slow down meaningful developments. In this sense, the present law of deregulation must also be understood: to increase user-friendliness and transparency of the legal order. That is why the European Commission's initiative to improve the legislation of the European Union under the title "Read and Better Regulations" has been taken up. The Austrian legislation has been critically examined with a view to avoiding rules. The preliminary work of the Committee 2 of the Austrian Convention was also taken into account. This is already the second step. We've heard it a few times today. Since 2000, 200 laws have been repealed. Why is such legal cleansing necessary? – This legalisation is closely linked to the general demand for better legislation at all, for it can be like with old clothes: If they no longer fit or have become unmodern after a while, you have to replace them or simply put them away. The purpose of this intelligible federal law is not exhausted in the mere repeal of obsolete legislation, but deregulation is understood in a quantitative and qualitative sense. However, we must bear in mind that, if legal cleansing is to have lasting success, it must go beyond one-off actions. For example, even the social democrats in Vienna have now decided to prepare a novel of the Vienna law on legalisation. For a long time, the ÖVP had to fight for the abolition in Vienna, for example, of the Spucknapf regulation for dance schools or the carpet block ban on the Danube Canal. Against some insanity, such as the provision that Buschenschaken only cooked hard If eggs are allowed to sell, the ÖVP did not arrive to this day, but it is known not to give up hope. That is why we are moving forward with this law with the best example.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e10917	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! Most of my previous speakers have dealt globally with this deregulation law. Allow me now to speak to you about Article 11, amendment of the Directive Law. It was in 1993 when, under the cooperation of Dr. Otto Keimel on the side of the ÖVP and Kurt Eder on the sides of the SPÖ, a indicative system was developed and an indicative value for each federal state was prescribed. These benchmarks have worked and will continue to function as an interest-rate limitation mechanism in the future. However, this system is not a rigid one, but it is adjusted annually to change the purchasing power in accordance with § 5 of the Directive and is thus also used as a guarantee of value. This is a coherent system, which has proved itself to be the best thing to do and generally enjoys recognition. The mothers and fathers of this law are to be congratulated! At that time – I was told about this by witnesses – there were great concerns on the part of the SPÖ about the new directive. It was required to set up an advisory board which could, under certain conditions, correct the new indicative values. However, these indicative values have proved to be such that this advisory board will fail in the future. As a positive result of this shattering, there must also be no mention of the significant resource savings, because, ladies and gentlemen, such a re-establishment of the indicative values would have been a very expensive process, which would also have brought high costs. Finally, I would like to say that in 1994 recommendations were maintained by several advisory committees and kept their effectiveness. So this withdrawal does not seem retroactive. It's an excellent law that doesn't lose any quality due to this deregulation. This slimming makes sense.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e11739	Mr Finz, State Secretary, spoke from the government bank. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e11760	Mr President. High National Council! This would have meant that, if we had done nothing, we would have suffered a loss of income of €80 million. [[Abg. Jakob Auer: That's it!]] Therefore, a new regulation of the bilateral agreement with Switzerland was necessary as a basis for this free movement agreement, which was concluded with the EU. In doing so, we have deleted the border regime without replacement and are now introducing a methodological change. So far, it has been true that on the Austrian side, a source tax of 3 percent was generally added to all income from non-self-employed work in relation to the method of avoiding double taxation. We now have a new system, which has fallen through this 3-percent limit, and instead we receive a refund of 12.5 percent of Swiss tax revenue. This corresponds roughly to the previous rise. However, the reason for this was not that we should change something in favour of Switzerland, but it was due to the fact that Switzerland has reached a new regulation with the EU. So this is a reaction from us, to which we do not see an alternative by which we could have avoided it. Rather, it was worth avoiding that we had tax outages. Thank you.

Matznetter.]] Among other things, the Austrian-Swiss double taxation agreement has proved to be urgently in need of revision, as a result of the entry into force of the EU-Switzerland free movement agreement. As a result of the entry into force of the bilateral agreement between Switzerland and the EU on the free movement of persons on 1 January 1989. In June 2002, the framework for the classification of Austrian workers as frontier workers changed. This could lead to significant shifts in tax revenue in Austria without revision of the double taxation agreement. The aim of the new agreement is to prevent another shift from Austria to Switzerland. The problem was solved by the fact that the Swiss wage income of employees resident in Austria is generally included in the accounting system, while at the same time paying an annual partial remuneration of 12.5 percent of the tax collected in Switzerland to the Republic of Austria in place of the previous limit of the Swiss tax entitlements of 3 percent on Austrian border incomes. [[Abg. Dr. Matznetter: Read the form!]] This scheme, which will result in an end to the previous frontier wound and its recovery to the Austrian tax network, may in the short term lead to reduced tax revenue in an estimated order of € 9 million per year. [[Abg. Dr. Matznetter: 25 percent!]] In the extreme case, however, without this scheme, it could result in a tax loss of €80 million. [[Abg. Dr. Matznetter: The Swiss are looking forward to... more!]] That means we lose in tax revenue, but without adjustment we lose almost tenfold. [[Abg. Dr. Matznetter: That's stupid!]] Furthermore, the mutual assistance should be extended. For the first time, a limited enforcement aid is also provided for in relation to Switzerland. This will eliminate inconsistencies, which have resulted mainly in those Austrian frontier workers who do not have an executive property in Austria. – This will improve the current very difficult situation of the financial offices. In addition, the work in the OECD to eliminate unfair tax practices revealed a need for revision of the double taxation agreement. Finally, revision negotiations have been initiated to bring about an OECD-compliant source tax relief in the case of licence fees, to ensure security of Austrian removal tax and to agree on an OECD-compliant agreement adjustment in the field of artist and sportsman taxation.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e19437	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I would like to congratulate the activists of the association “Coole Schule”. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] Florentine Frantz and Igor Mitschka are also here. Welcome to the High House! I am really deeply impressed by your commitment, your wealth of ideas and even your political maturity. [[New congratulations to the SPÖ and the Greens.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Even if this is particularly talented young people: You are the living proof of how exactly young people know what they want – often better and more than some of you love in politics. The proposals that have been drawn up are probably thought-out and address directly the wounded points of the current school system. They are also practically feasible with a portion of good will. A very important concern is the democratization of the school for pupils. Students of all – and not only those of the higher school levels – should be allowed to choose class speakers who, in turn, can choose between school speakers and national school speakers. I also like the idea of the school parliament, which is to be composed of the class speakers as well as parent and teacher representatives. The special and appealing part of this proposal is that this body, unlike the current school community committee, should have a majority or, at least, a veto right for pupils. Of course, this school Parliament is supposed to have real power to decide. The further proposals of the ‘Coole School’ association are also truly worthy of representation: the use of new teaching and learning methods, more project work, more practice-oriented teaching, excursions. – Frontal lessons in the 50-minute cycle are simply considered to be no longer contemporary. The students wish for more reform pedagogical methods and, in order to be optimally prepared for life, also new subjects and more time for social learning. English should already be taught in the elementary school, which is of course also useful, because we know that language skills are the greatest in young years. Individual promotion, also fear and stress management should have a very special importance – rather than pressure by the drying of class repetitions. The demand for increased psychological care in schools seems very important to me, especially in view of the increasingly frequent acts of violence in schools and the increasing problems of addiction. The young people simply need a dock, not only for school, but also for human problems, and of course not instead, but in addition to the parents' home. Especially in the most important phase of life, the young people in the school are entrusted not only to mediate knowledge but also to mediate personality. Of course, holistic learning also requires appropriate framework conditions. In addition to an improved training of teachers, new teaching times are also required, afternoon care and the possibility of a free all day school – of course with relaxation phases and food. Very innovative is also the proposal to create a new school system with the aim of creating common regional centres for children between five and 15 years of age, with two-language classes, multi-stage classes in primary schools and course systems. There are really a lot of very interesting ideas, some of which were already objects of applications, partly but completely new. At any rate, however, they are worthy of having serious discussions so that the school really becomes a “cool school ” as soon as possible.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e19519	The next item is Mrs Fuhrmann's speech. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e19540	Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Mr President. I too would like to thank the ‘Coole School’ association in principle for the commitment shown by the representatives. It is indeed very impressive – and I say this as a former student representative – when young people, besides their schooling, which is also very strenuous, also volunteer. I myself took part in the student congress and took part in the discussions on the topics that have been worked out there and also answered questions on the ground. However, I have to add the following, and I have to make the most of it in principle – also because of the fact that the school is approaching, and even in all the federal states, that is, the elections of the national student representatives are about to take place: There is already a legal student representation, which is basically included in the assessment of all school laws. Students thus have the opportunity to discuss their concerns on-the-spot – also in discussions with the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs. Nevertheless, it is positive to mention that an association also aims to discuss school policy agendas and also to think about how to make school everyday even better. We therefore invited both the legal student representation and the association within the framework of a teaching committee, and I am personally very glad that it was possible in this way to bring together various initiatives. I believe that the compromise we have reached in this committee, namely that, in the sense of the student representation, the work or composition of the legal student representation is in principle questioned and analysed, is a very good one. I would therefore also invite the association “Coole Schule” to take part in the legal student representation. [[Abg. Mandak: If they can't be chosen because they're too young!]] You all have the opportunity to register as school speakers or school speakers. You also have the opportunity to continue candidates for the national student representation and finally to be active within the framework of the federal student representation. There are already candidate groups, such as the Austrian Student Union, but also the “Action of Critical Students”. I believe that if more initiatives are taken and more campaigning, then this is only fertilizing. However, I would also like to focus on the proposals made by ‘Coole School’ and to make a few critical comments, which I must say, above all on the point – and I say this again as a former student representative – that it is intended to abolish the School Forum, i.e. also the School Community Committee. At the time, we fought very intensively and stubbornly in the student representation for the possibility of creating a legal body consisting of teachers, pupils and parents' representatives, where every group, each group receives equal number of mandates or votes in the body, so that it is not possible for teachers to exceed pupils or parents teachers et cetera. And I would consider it a step back if, suddenly, the trend was to let this school partnership, which was hard to develop in school and which has also become an initiative at the national and federal level, sink back into the ground. I believe that much has gone on in the area of school partnership, but also in the field of school autonomy, and I would also like to thank the Federal Minister for this. To give just a few examples: In recent years, we have decided on new behavioral agreements, where a new path has been targeted towards an agreement culture away from the order culture. Teachers, students and parents now finally have the opportunity, within the framework of autonomy, to become active and shape them on the spot in their school itself. However, I can also mention another example where the student representation was very initiative and where a demand which was then decided to go back to the student representation, namely the reform of the matura year. That the semester certificate has been abolished and the final school level is made more efficient, which has been very well discussed here in Parliament, is based on a demand from the Federal Schools' Office. This was a very important initiative of the student representatives, in which one also sees that they find belong here. I believe that in the future it will be even more important to understand and hear the interests of the students, and I believe that we are sure that school democracy will continue to be lived and all school partners will be involved in decision-making processes. – I continue to ask for support for this.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e20155	Mr President. High house! I believe that especially in Austria, the school partnership has a very good basis that the active school partnership is a quality feature of our schools. As one of the few countries in Europe, we have legislated this school partnership and have constantly developed this school partnership. There are school partnership bodies at all levels, and I think it is very important to work well with the school partners. Together with the school partners, we have set a path from an order culture to an agreement culture. The behavioural agreements are carried out by the school partners. Together with the school partners, we have realized more freedom of choice and action in schools. We have launched new projects in an active school partnership. I am very grateful to the parents, the teachers' representatives, the pupils' and pupils' representatives. For example, they have set up the “Performance Map ” project, where pupils’ achievements are confirmed outside the witness, such as when a class speaker was when a school speaker was when one of them was particularly involved in a project. This shows what skills and competences a young person has. I would also like to thank you very much for the “Faire School” project – a project that the school partners have developed, that the school partners will carry together and that will lead to a fair school, taking into account all the needs of the school partners, as far as possible, without limiting others in their freedom. I mean, we also have a best-practice model in Europe with this model. But it can all be improved. That is why I think it is very reasonable for us to carry out an evaluation of the current legislation of the Federal Law, which originates in 1990; if we also make adjustments proposed by the school partners. Therefore, as is stated in the motion for a resolution, we will set up a working group with the representatives of the Austrian Federal Schools' Office and, if the evaluation shows that there is need for amendment, we will also submit the necessary legislative proposal. The Austrian school partnership is good, the Austrian school partnership lives, and we will continue to work well together in the school partner area.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e20966	We're on our way to the 11th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker was Mr Ledolter. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e20994	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! This Convention, whose treatment the President has just begun, is about the accession of the ten EU acceding countries to the European Union in May of 2004 as well as the transfer of legislation, in so far as these candidate countries are to ratify this Convention: on the law applicable to contractual debt and on the additional protocols governing the interpretation of this Convention by the European Court of Justice. By the way, there is a 1988 Judicature. Austria ratified this matter in 1998. This now sounds a bit bulky, but it is clear that the acquisition of this right also entails effects, namely the harmonisation of the conflict rules on contractual debt, measures that are in the interest of Austria, because they create or increase legal certainty and, of course, improve economic relations between the EU Member States and make them legally sound. As I have already mentioned, these individual Contracting Parties' Accession Conventions are necessary, whereby Member States are also bound by uniform conflict rules on contractual debt, which clearly defines that in all EU Member States, the issues which have been very doubtful so far, namely the right to contractual relations with foreign countries, are to be solved in a uniform manner. This, ladies and gentlemen, is an essential contribution to the establishment of a functioning internal market, but on the other hand it is also a complement on judicial competence, on the recognition and enforcement of decisions in civil and commercial matters. Forum shopping is stopped, i.e. the search, in which legal background a possible causa, a matter has to produce the better result in terms of a judgment. In principle, it is clear that the same material law must be applied in all countries. This is another example of successful judicial policy, just as successful foreign policy has been made by our Federal Chancellor, who has agreed with President Bush to abolish Guantánamo.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21409	We come to points 12 to 14 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker is Mr Schöls. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21436	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! This item on the agenda will, among other things, amend the Statute of the Hague Conference, a matter which has already been dealt with unanimously in the Committee. The Hague Conference on Private International Law was founded in 1893 for the purpose of standardizing international private law, and Austria-Hungary was one of the founding states. At present, 65 states are members of the Hague Conference. The Hague Conference has drawn up numerous important conventions that have gained worldwide importance, such as the Hague Convention of 25. October 1980 on the civil aspects of international child abduction and the Hague Convention of 29 October 1980. The Council adopted a resolution on the protection of children and cooperation in the field of international adoption on 15 May 1993. Cooperation between the Hague Conference and the European Community is becoming increasingly intense. The European Commission has long participated as an observer. As regards the materials which fall within the competence of the European Community, the European Commission has exclusive powers of representation, it was necessary to comply with this legal situation by including the European Community as a member of the Hague Conference. In order to make this possible, this change was necessary. This amendment shall take effect in accordance with the current nature. 12 of the Statute shall be objectively in force if it has been approved by two thirds of the members. As soon as this is the case, the Hague Conference will convene a meeting of the General Affairs and Policy Council to decide on the inclusion of the European Community as a member.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21721	Mrs Stadlbauer is speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21742	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! High house! With the 1998 Convention on Criminal Law and the 1999 Convention on Civil Law, the Council of Europe has set up two international framework agreements on anti-corruption, and today's decision on the Civil Law Convention on Corruption creates a legal instrument of the Council of Europe. Legal standards in the fight against corruption are thus created. Contracting States must now have a certain minimum standard of corruption rules. The term “corruption” is defined, damage to corruption is regulated, appropriate evidence is allowed in civil processes and so on. The implementation of the anti-corruption conventions as well as the other instruments of the Council of Europe are monitored by the Group of States against corruption, in short GRECO. GRECO is an extra-established group of states and has 40 members. Of the EU Member States, only Austria and Italy are missing. That is to say, we are implementing relatively late; the resolution has already been adopted in 1999. This gives the impression of a rather slow approach, and in order to counter this impression, we naturally agree with the agreement, because it is very important for us to take decisive action against the phenomenon of corruption. Unfortunately, we can see that corruption is spreading in some countries of the Council of Europe. Corruption always poses a threat to the rule of law, democracy and, ultimately, human rights. Corruption is the abuse of entrusted power for its own advantage. An effective means of combating corruption is transparency and disclosure. As in so many areas, the work of relevant NGOs is a good measure and a provider of information about the situation, and it is worth looking at them. In the field of corruption, Transparency International, in short TI, is a non-governmental organization dedicated to the fight against corruption worldwide. Since 1995, TI has been collecting data for the International Corruption Index in 146 countries. This index indicates the perception of corruption. According to this index, 106 out of 146 countries reach less than five out of ten possible points. 60 countries are even below 3 out of 10 possible points, indicating deep-rooted corruption. Most of the countries at the top are economically strong, such as Iceland, Finland, New Zealand and Denmark, and the lowest are mostly the economically weak countries, such as Chad, Bangladesh and Turkmenistan. If we look at the situation at EU level, we can see that there are major problems in the area of corruption, particularly in the countries that might join Romania and Bulgaria in 2007. But there is also progress. They stand out in Romania, and the European Union's pressure has caused a lot there. For example, the legal system is reformed and the independence of judges and lawyers is strengthened. But there is still much to be done – on this point I have to give my colleague, Partik-Pablé – but I think that we are taking a positive step in this decision. In conclusion: Disclosure is the best method of fighting corruption. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21831	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! After the previous debates, I am pleased to note that the House will obviously adopt all three conventions unanimously here and would like to thank me for this. As regards the individual Conventions, it is still clear to the House whether Austria will still need further implementation under these Conventions. As far as the Government template concerning the Statutes of the Hague Conference on Private International Law is concerned, this is “only” – a reference mark; please, not to understand that, this is a very far-reaching measure, which is very important – a legislative change so that the European Community can join the Hague Conference and actively cooperate. We do not need any further action in Austria on the basis of the ratification of this Convention. In connection with the Civil Law Convention on Corruption, I would like to do the following: Mrs Stadlbauer is right when she says that this has been going on for a very long time since 1999 – that is, for seven years. However, I am still very glad that this agreement is finally before the House for ratification. This Convention obliges the Contracting States to have a certain minimum standard of civil and civil procedure, labour law and civil liability rules. We have already made provision in Austria for no further legal action to be taken on the basis of this Convention. We are now joining this agreement, which is taken note of by the Council of Europe with special goodwill. We are, as Mrs Stadlbauer correctly said, the last EU Member State to ratify this Convention, and this is not necessarily a famous one, but better the latter than the last. It is important to me that we now join this agreement. Above all, I believe that this is an important signal to the new Member States, where, as has already been mentioned by my previous speakers, corruption is a central issue in achieving the objectives of the acquis communautaire. It is particularly important that we in Europe stand together in the fight against corruption and act together. I believe that this agreement is an important step. The International Convention on the Control of Nuclear Terrorism is to be said: In terms of content, this Convention obliges States Parties to punish acts of nuclear terrorism. Cooperation shall be provided for in the law enforcement of persons who have committed or suspected criminal acts falling within the scope of the Convention. We have decided here in the House on environmental criminal law and, as a result, we have provided for all measures to transpose this Convention into national law, so that there is no further need for implementation on the basis of this Convention in Austria. That's how we took care of this. You will decide the formal conditions here today. In any case, Austria is prepared to implement all three conventions in our country on the basis of the existing legal order. – Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21886	The next item is the report by Mr Ing. Kapeller. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21908	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Although the World Cup game starts in five minutes, I would like to say something about postmodern terrorism. Today, the ratification of the present agreement brings a gap. Post-communist terrorism means, on the part of the executive, the fight against ABC weapons that could reach in the hands of terrorist organisations. The last ten years have shown that this is possible. Until then, it has been assumed that ABC weapons will hardly be found in non-governmental organizations, because it is financially very difficult to acquire or produce such weapons because it requires enormous expertise. Since the attack in Tokyo in 1995 by the Aum sect, it has been empirically proven that there are three organizations – the Aum sects, Al-Qaida and the Chechen rebels – that are trying massively to produce bombs et cetera in the field of nuclear technology. There are various threat scenarios in the field of nuclear terrorist actions. It is assumed that the completion of an “A-battle bomb” will hardly be possible, because of the fact that one does not get so much enriched uranium in order to produce such a bomb and does not have the necessary know-how. The second threat scenario consists in sabotage acts both inside and outside which are aimed at nuclear installations, especially from the air. The real danger, however, exists in the so-called dirty bombs. This means that this bomb is conventionally lit, but is contaminated with radioactive material. These threat scenarios must be stopped by international police cooperation. That's also possible. No one can imagine the psychological effects if, for example, a so-called dirty bomb would be lit in the Vienna subway network. It would not cause many people on site to suffer damage or material damage, but the psychological effect, namely not being able to imagine that in contaminated buildings, professional life or personal life will ever take place again, would have unexpected dimensions. That would have disastrous consequences. It is therefore essential to sign this Agreement. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e21980	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to turn to the Civil Law Convention on Corruption. As a member of the Council of Europe, I am particularly pleased that we are finally ratifying this Convention today – as the last country. [[President Dr. Khol will again take over the presidency.]] This is important because increasing corruption poses a threat to the rule of law, democracy and human rights. But not only that, it also threatens the economic development of the countries concerned and affects the proper functioning of economies. In general, corruption leads to a decrease in the performance of organisations or a deterioration in quality, but the contributions to be paid for it are increasing. According to the World Bank, on average every person has to pay about 7 percent of his work for corruption damage. In the field of public administration, corruption leads to high material but also enormous immaterial damage and the loss of confidence of citizens. For example, contracts may be awarded to companies that provide more expensive or higher quality services than those that would be selected in an objective and transparent tender. The benefits granted to officials are usually taken into account in the accounts. Therefore, benefits will be charged, either not at all or not to the designated extent. The financial burden must ultimately be borne by taxpayers. “Using public positions for the private advantage is unwelcome” – a definition by Hans Herbert von Arnim, “Corruption, Networks in Politics, Offices and Economy”. – I also wanted to provide a definition for “corruption”. These agreements – not only the Convention on the fight against civil corruption, but also the so-called GRECO Convention – are very important for Europe as a whole in economic terms, as they will bring confidence to all signatory states, including Austria, in the case of potential investors. This increase in confidence is also intended to produce positive employment effects. It has already been said here that Austria is in 10th place compared to the other Member States of the Council of Europe or to the other countries of the world – the 159 surveyed – and still in front of Germany, but not in comparison with Finland, Iceland or Sweden. So, it's not like Austria would be totally corruption-free. I would now like to dedicate myself to the second agreement, the GRECO agreement, which is also being debated here, namely the agreement on the establishment of the group of States against corruption. This represents an important step towards a Europe-wide transparent and enforceable anti-corruption policy. It is an instrument for reviewing the contractual obligations. It may make recommendations to the evaluated member and make the publication, which is an important instrument for complying with certain provisions, in the event of failure to comply with them. We will take a close look at the evaluation reports – and these are planned annually – by GRECO, and then we will see whether the Austrian legal instruments are sufficient to really comply with the anti-corruption rules. I would like to point out, Mrs Minister, that the 1999 Criminal Law Convention on Corruption is still pending ratification. Another note: I have seen with joy that the Convention against trafficking in human beings has now arrived as a government template. I have already pointed out in the plenary debate here in Parliament, on the occasion of the birthday of our 50-year membership of the Council of Europe, that it would be very important for Austria to sign this Convention. If we were to do this now – and so it looks like it; I hope that this Convention will be dealt with at the next judicial committee meeting – then we would not be at the last third of the Signatories, then we would not be the last ones, like now, but then we would be the ones who signed this Convention at the very beginning. Trafficking in human beings is a very central issue to combat. I hope that we will deal with this issue in Parliament very soon. I'd really be very pleased. Thank you very much, Mrs. Minister. I would also like to thank Mrs Plassnik and Mr Winkler, Minister of State, who, on the occasion of the celebration of the 50th anniversary in the Council of Europe, I was able to mention it. As has been said, this Convention has now been presented as a government template, and I am very pleased. Then: Trafficking in human beings is something that needs to be fought – no matter where it happens! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e22246	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The Media Act regulates a socially most sensitive matter: On the one hand, it is about fundamental personality rights – about their protection – and on the other hand about freedom of expression, freedom of the media and the right of all citizens to information. The legislator, but as a result, also the law-makers have a difficult path to take in this area. A fundamental change in the law can and should not be quickly broken over the knee in this matter. Nevertheless, ladies and gentlemen, there are individual areas that should be regulated from our point of view. In concrete terms, it is now about securing the protection of journalistic sources on the basis of a decision of the Supreme Court. I should like to point out that, according to the case law of the European Court of Human Rights, the protection of journalistic sources is one of the basic conditions for freedom of the press guaranteed by Article 10 of the Convention on Human Rights. What's this about? – It is about the fact that in media crime proceedings also accused media owners, publishers, media employees and employees of a media company or media service fall under the protection of editorial secrecy. The current regulation only protects witnesses in a media crime procedure, but not accused journalists. Therefore, one could happen: The opening of criminal proceedings against a cover journalist – and this is the result of this above-mentioned decision – the possibility of criminal procedure including the confiscation of documents. We believe that the editorial secret – and I emphasize: only in media offences! – should also be extended to the accused. High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! We from the Socialist Group also believe that the media law should be subject to a fundamental reform, in particular taking into account the new communication technologies. In this context, I would like to refer to the discussion which is currently taking place in Germany. We therefore believe that in Austria, during the next legislative period, an interdisciplinary committee should be set up by all the groups concerned, the political parties as well as the scientific committee on media law, which is to draw up a fundamental reform of the media procedure. We should get away from the criminal law side to a civil law solution to this problem. But regardless of what we believe, and I look at the chairman of the Committee on Justice, we may still be able to agree during this period, so that this problem, as set out in my request, can still be resolved jointly. – I thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e22614	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Each year, reports are also prepared by the Ministry of Finance on the damage or seizures that have occurred. It is, of course, only a small part of the actual damage. If one sees that in 2005 180 000 counterfeited articles were picked up with a total value of over €33 million, and if one considers the enormous development in the years before – from €6 million in 2003 to €11 million in 2004 and then €30 million in 2005 – then one also sees the rapid development in this area. An example of this area, which concerns Austria and has provided headlines in the last two years, is the company Doppelmayr-Lifte in Vorarlberg. It has delivered about 30 lifts to China, but there are now more than 200 “double-mayr” lifts – under quotation marks –; it has already exported such lifts to Vietnam and Africa, but they cause enormous security concerns in these countries, so that this world trading company, which is also a leader in production, is internationally damaged. It can be seen that this includes the Austrian view of the need for action. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: The foreign trade agencies have huge problems!]] Wherever there is market leader, wherever there are leaders in market and economic policy, copy and piracy are applied. This must be prevented, and all we can do is also necessary. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e22871	Last word in this first reading: Mr Eßl. He too speaks for up to 2 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00154_d7e22892	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It is important to amend the product piracy law. This is the Federal Act which lays down additional rules on the procedure of customs authorities in the transport of goods which infringe a right to intellectual property. This law dates back to 2004. As we have already heard, there is now a four-party agreement that you want to go in a certain direction and change this. At that time, it was necessary to implement the so-called EC Regulation on Piracy and to adopt supplementary national regulations. This law lays down the measures to be taken by the customs administration and creates an instrument allowing the customs authorities to withdraw goods which are subject to the right of protection as early as possible from the market. I believe that if this change is now being implemented, we have a modern law with which we can live well again for a few years. Thank you very much.
